Sub.: Submission of Annual Report for the financial year 2025-26 under Regulation 34 of SEBI (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations 2015 
Dear Sir/Madam, 
In terms of the requirements of Regulation 34(1) of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, we are submitting herewith the 8th Annual Report of the Company, the Business Responsibility and 
Sustainability Report and Notice of 8th Annual General Meeting (AGM) for the financial year 2025-26, which is being 
sent to the shareholders by electronic mode. 
The 8th Annual General Meeting of the Company will be held on Friday, August 07, 2026 at 11:00 a.m. through Video 
Conferencing/ Other Audio Visual means (VC/OAVM) in accordance, with the relevant circulars issued by Ministry 
of Corporate Affairs and Securities and Exchange Board of India (SEBI). 
Pursuant to Section 108 of the Companies Act, 2013 read with Rule 20 of the Companies (Management and 
Administration) Rules, 2014 and Regulation 44 of the SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) 
Regulations, 2015, the Company is pleased to provide its members with the remote e-voting facility to cast their votes 
electronically on the resolutions mentioned in the AGM Notice using the electronic voting platform provided by MUFG 
Intime India Private Limited (MIIPL). The voting rights of members shall be in proportion to the shares held by them, 
as on the cut-off date i.e. Friday, July 31, 2026. 
The remote e-voting period commences on Tuesday, August 04, 2026 at 09:00 a.m. and ends on Thursday, August 06, 
2026 at 05:00 p.m. The remote e-voting module shall be disabled by MIIPL for voting thereafter. In addition, the facility 
for voting through electronic voting system shall also be made available at the AGM and the members participating in 
AGM through VC/OAVM, who have not already cast their vote by remote e-voting shall be able to exercise their rights 
in the meeting. 
The Annual Report containing the AGM Notice is also uploaded on the Company’s website viz. 
https://corporate.celloworld.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/CWL_AR25-26.pdf   
Kindly take the same on your record. 
Thanking you. 
Yours faithfully, 
For Cello World Limited 
 
 
Hemangi Trivedi 
Company Secretary and Compliance Officer 
Encl: A/a

Annual Report 2025-26 2 Cello World Limited 3Corporate overview
REWRITING GROWTH  
WITH A TRUSTED LEGACYTheme Introduction
Some legacies are built over time. Others return with 
even greater purpose.
For over six decades, Cello has been an integral part 
of millions of Indian households, earning trust through 
quality, innovation, and products that seamlessly become 
part of everyday life. This enduring legacy has been 
shaped by an unwavering commitment to understanding 
consumer aspirations and consistently delivering value 
across generations. 
FY 2025-26 marks a defining chapter in this remarkable 
journey. Return of iconic Cello brand in the writing 
instruments and stationery category is more than the 
reacquisition of a celebrated name—it is the homecoming 
of a legacy that has long been synonymous with trust, 
familiarity, and excellence. By bringing the brand back 
into its portfolio, Cello World has reunited one of India’s 
most recognised consumer brands with its original vision, 
unlocking new opportunities for growth while reinforcing 
its emotional connect with consumers. The management 
also highlighted that Cello brand continues to enjoy 
strong consumer recall and trust, and that the Company 
intends to leverage its manufacturing and distribution 
strengths to unlock its full potential. 
This milestone is complemented by continued 
investments in manufacturing excellence, premium 
product innovation, digital channels, and distribution 
capabilities, enabling Cello World to strengthen its 
leadership across consumerware, writing instruments, 
and furniture. Each initiative reflects a clear ambition—not 
merely to expand the business, but to elevate the value it 
creates for customers, partners, and stakeholders. 
“Rewriting Growth with a Trusted Legacy ” encapsulates 
this defining moment. It reflects a future where heritage 
fuels innovation, trust inspires transformation, and every 
milestone builds upon decades of consumer confidence. 
As the Cello legacy comes full circle, our Company is not 
simply continuing its story—it is rewriting its next chapter 
with renewed purpose, stronger capabilities, and a 
vision to shape the future of India’s consumer products 
landscape.

Annual Report 2025-26 14 Cello World Limited 15Corporate overview
LEADING THE NEXT CHAPTER 
OF GROWTHChairman & Managing Director’s Message
It gives me immense pleasure to present the Annual 
Report of Cello World Limited for the financial year 
2025-26. During the year, we continued to strengthen 
our foundation by combining our trusted legacy with 
innovation, operational excellence, and a sharp focus on 
delivering long-term value.
A defining milestone of the year was return of iconic Cello 
brand to our writing instruments and stationery portfolio. 
This strategic acquisition reinforces our commitment to 
rewriting growth with a trusted legacy, strengthening 
our leadership in the category while creating new 
opportunities for innovation, market expansion, and 
sustainable growth.
Resilience in a Changing World
The global economy during FY 2025-26 remained shaped 
by geopolitical uncertainties, volatile commodity prices, 
and evolving trade dynamics, creating a challenging 
operating environment for businesses worldwide. Despite 
these headwinds, India continued to outperform most 
major economies, recording a GDP growth of 7.7%, 
supported by resilient domestic consumption, sustained 
infrastructure investments, and continued policy reforms.
The year also witnessed heightened input cost pressures, 
driven by fluctuations in the prices of key raw materials, 
elevated energy costs, and supply chain disruptions. 
Against this backdrop, we remained focused on 
operational agility, disciplined execution, and customer-
centric innovation. Despite the challenging environment, 
we delivered revenue of `2,324 crore, reflecting a 9% 
year-on-year growth, while EBITDA stood at `526 crore 
and Profit After Tax reached `332 crore. These results 
underscore resilience of our diversified portfolio, the 
strength of our trusted brands, and our ability to navigate 
evolving market conditions while creating sustainable 
long-term value.
Operational Highlights
Our commitment to strengthening long-term capabilities 
remained at the forefront during the year. We accelerated 
our manufacturing expansion by commissioning new 
production lines for steel bottles and select consumer 
categories at our Rajasthan facility, with additional 
capacities becoming operational to support future growth. The successful merger of Wimplast Limited 
with Cello World marked another significant milestone, 
creating stronger synergies across our Consumerware 
and Moulded Furniture businesses while enhancing 
operational efficiencies.
We also continued to realign our business for evolving 
consumer behaviour by streamlining our product portfolio 
and strengthening our distribution strategy. The growing 
contribution of modern trade, e-commerce and quick 
commerce, which now accounts for nearly 17% of our 
revenue, complements our robust General Trade network 
and expands our market reach. While the glassware 
business operated at around 60% capacity utilisation due 
to pricing pressures from low-cost imports, we remain 
confident in its long-term growth potential and continue to 
invest in building a stronger, more competitive business.
Financial Highlights
Despite challenging operating environment, Cello World 
delivered another year of resilient growth, with revenue 
from operations increasing around 9% to `2,324 crore 
in Cello World. Growth was supported by the strong 
performance of Writing Instruments business, along with 
steady contributions from Opalware, Glassware, and select 
Consumerware categories. While higher operating costs 
associated with the new glassware plant and steel bottle 
manufacturing facility, coupled with subdued demand in 
select categories, impacted profitability, we maintained a 
healthy financial position with EBITDA of `526 Crore and 
Profit After Tax of `332 Crore.
A key highlight of the year was our highest-ever 
quarterly revenue of `654 crore in Q4 FY26, reflecting 
an encouraging recovery in business momentum. The 
gradual ramp-up of new manufacturing capacities and 
contribution from Cello stationery brand acquisition 
further strengthened our growth trajectory, positioning us 
well for sustained value creation in the years ahead.
Reacquisition of the Iconic Cello Brand
A defining milestone during the year was the reacquisition 
of iconic Cello brand for writing instruments and stationery, 
bringing back one of India’s most trusted brands to our 
portfolio after its divestment in 2015. This strategic move 
has enabled us to establish a complementary dual-brand portfolio comprising Cello and UNOMAX, significantly 
enhancing our presence across customer segments and 
price points. 
The acquisition has already begun contributing to 
business performance, with the Writing Instruments 
category delivering 64% year-on-year growth in Q4 
2025-26, supported by the initial contribution from Cello 
stationery brand. Building on this momentum, we remain 
confident of scaling the business further and targeting 
writing instruments revenue of over `500 Crore in 2026-
27, reinforcing our long-term growth ambitions.
Way Forward
As we look ahead, Cello World confident in it’s ability to 
build on our strong foundation and create sustainable 
long-term value. Cello World is confident to strengthen  
core categories, accelerating premiumisation, expanding 
manufacturing capabilities, and enhancing our presence 
across emerging retail channels, including e-commerce 
and quick commerce.
Return of iconic Cello brand to writing instruments 
portfolio, ramp-up of our new manufacturing capacities, 
and the integration of Wimplast position us to unlock new 
avenues of growth and operational synergies. Cello World 
will continue to invest in innovation, deepen consumer 
engagement, and improve operational efficiencies while 
maintaining prudent capital allocation and financial 
discipline.
Vote of Thanks
On behalf of the Board, I extend my heartfelt gratitude to 
our shareholders for their continued trust and confidence 
in our journey. I also sincerely thank our customers, 
channel partners, suppliers, and business associates for 
their unwavering support and partnership.
I am deeply appreciative of our employees, whose 
passion, dedication, and commitment continue to drive 
our success. My sincere thanks also go to our Board of 
Directors for their invaluable guidance and strategic 
counsel, and to the Government authorities, regulatory 
bodies, lenders, and all other stakeholders for their 
continued encouragement and support.
As we move forward, our committed to rewriting growth 
with a trusted legacy, creating sustainable value and 
delivering excellence for generations to come.
Warm regards,
Pradeep Ghisulal Rathod 
Chairman & Managing Director
Despite a challenging operating environment, we delivered 
another year of resilient growth, with revenue from operations 
increasing around 9% to `2,324 Crore in FY26.“
”

Annual Report 2025-26 18 Cello World Limited 19Corporate overview
THE FOUNDATION OF 
SUSTAINABLE GROWTHOur Manufacturing Capabilities
Cello’s manufacturing engine is one of it’s core competitive strengths, enabling it to manufacture a diverse range of 
products in-house, respond quickly to demand, and maintain tight control over quality and its supply chain.
PAN-INDIA MANUFACTURING FOOTPRINT 
Our manufacturing operations are spread across 14 state-of-the-art facilities located in Daman, Haridwar, Baddi, Chennai, 
Kolkata, and Rajasthan (Falna). Cello is the only domestic consumer products company with a presence across all material 
types — plastics, steel, and glass — to also have an in-house glassware manufacturing unit in India.
EXPANDING OUR MANUFACTURING CAPACITY 
Capacity expansion remains central to Cello’s growth strategy, with a 
sustained pipeline of investments across glassware, steel, opalware, 
and writing instruments — aimed at reducing import dependence, 
improving cost competitiveness, and supporting the addition of the 
Cello brand to the writing instruments portfolio.
Key capacity expansion initiatives
l Glassware manufacturing facility in Rajasthan (Falna):   
 commissioned with an annual installed capacity of ~20,000  
 tonnes, being operationalized in a phased manner; current  
 utilization stands at ~60%. The facility houses European-made  
 machinery enabling high productivity along with pr ecision in  
 design and is expected to lower C ello’s dependence   
 on imported glasswar e.
l Steel ware capacity: installed at the Rajasthan (Falna) facility  
 to manufacture indigenous steel tablewar e products, reducing  
 reliance on imports and third-party OEM sourcing.
l Planned capacity incre ases: across plastic products, insulated  
 ware, moulded furniture, and writing instruments and stationery  
 (including the Cello br and), to be undertaken in a phased   
 manner as demand scales.14

KEY PEOPLE INITIATIVES 
IN FY 2025-26Our People
BUILDING A HIGH-PERFORMANCE 
CULTURE
To strengthen execution and accelerate sustainable 
growth, Cello World Limited and its subsidiaries have 
implemented a structured Performance Management 
Framework across all functions. Clear Key Result Areas 
(KRAs) and Key Performance Indicators (KPIs) are being 
cascaded from our strategic priorities to every business 
unit and individual role, ensuring alignment with 
organizational objectives.
The framework emphasizes measurable outcomes, 
accountability, operational excellence, innovation, cost 
optimization, quality, customer satisfaction, and people 
development. Regular performance reviews, transparent 
goal tracking, and merit-based recognition will foster a 
culture of continuous improvement and high performance.
By aligning individual contributions with business 
objectives, we aim to enhance productivity, improve 
decision-making, strengthen leadership accountability, 
and create long-term value for customers, employees, 
and shareholders.Key Focus Areas

The initiative aimed to encourage greater participation

POSH AWARENESS & CERTIFICATION - 
BUILDING A SAFE AND INCLUSIVE 
WORKPLACE
At Cello World, we successfully implemented a 100% 
POSH (Prevention of Sexual Harassment) Awareness and 
Certification Programme across all manufacturing plants, 
warehouses, sales offices, and corporate locations through 
the Skill India Digital Learning Initiative. The programme 
covered all employees, including staff, executives, and 
workers, reinforcing our commitment to providing a safe, 
respectful with inclusive workplace environment.
By leveraging a standardized online certification 
platform, our Company ensured consistent awareness 
of POSH legislation, workplace ethics, employee rights 
and responsibilities, along with grievance redressal 
mechanisms. This initiative strengthens our culture 
of dignity, respect, and accountability while ensuring 
compliance with statutory requirements by promoting 
a workplace where every employee can contribute with 
confidence.Key Focus Areas
l 100% completion and certifica tion across all locations 
l Coverage of employees and workers across   
 manufacturing, sales,  and corporate functions 
l Delivered through the Skill India Digital Learning   
 Platform 
l Reinforced awareness of POSH guidelines, workplace  
 ethics, and employee responsibilities 
l Strengthened compliance, inclusion, and a culture of  
 respect and safety across the organization

THROUGH SOCIAL IMPACTSocial Impact

sustainable impact on society through focused Corporate

organization contributed `845.36 lakhs towards education,

commitment to responsible and inclusive growth.
Our CSR investments are directed towards strengthening 
educational institutions, supporting underprivileged 
communities, promoting grassroots sports, improving 
healthcare access, and contributing to the welfare of 
defense personnel and public institutions. Through 
strategic partnerships with credible organizations,

Annual Report 2025-26 24 Cello World Limited 25Corporate overview
A GREENER LEGACY  
FOR TOMORROWEnvironmental Stewardship
Cello follows a structured process for reusing Low-Density (LD) plastic waste 
generated during production. This waste is channelled to authorised recyclers 
for conversion into polybags, which are then repurposed across various 
applications — supporting circular economy practices and sustainable resource 
utilisation. Similarly, opalware and plastic waste generated during production is 
granulated and reintroduced into the manufacturing process, reducing overall 
material wastageWaste Management
Cello adopts a comprehensive waste management 
strategy centred on reducing, reusing and recycling 
waste, supported by process optimisation, efficient 
machinery, and cross-functional innovation. Hazardous 
waste — primarily polyurethane, the only hazardous 
waste generated at its facilities — is safely collected and 
disposed of through the certified incineration process 
of Gujarat Enviro Protection & Infrastructure Ltd. (GEPIL), 
in adherence to pollution control regulations. Non-
hazardous waste is responsibly managed through official 
partnerships with the Omnibus Industrial Development 
Corporation (OIDC), Daman, and the Metal Scrap Trade 
Corporation (MSTC), Baroda, ensuring transparent and 
regulation-aligned disposal through periodic auctions.
Water Stewardship
Our Company, being primarily engaged in trading 
activities, does  not  have manufacturing  operations of 
its own.  However,  through its manufacturing subsidiary, 
the Company remains committed to responsible 
water management. The subsidiary is strengthening 
its water discharge monitoring mechanisms and 
reported no instances of water-related pollution or non-
compliance during the year. It continues to enhance 
its water management practices in line with applicable 
environmental regulations.
Emission Reduction
While the Company is primarily engaged in trading 
activities, its manufacturing subsidiary has commissioned 
rooftop solar power installations across its manufacturing 
units in Daman, supporting renewable energy adoption 
and reducing carbon emissions. The subsidiary also 
reintroduces opalware and plastic waste generated during 
production into the manufacturing process, promoting 
resource efficiency and reducing the consumption of 
virgin materials.
Waste category Management approach Partner
Hazardous waste 
(Polyurethane)Certified incineration GEPIL
Non-hazardous waste Responsible disposal via 
periodic auctionsOIDC Daman & MSTC Baroda
LD plastic / opalware & plastic scrap Recycling and in-process reuse Authorised Recyclers

1. The Ministry of Corporate Affairs (“MCA”) has, 
vide its General Circular dated September 22, 
2025 read together with circulars dated April 8, 
2020, April 13, 2020, May 5, 2020, January 13, 
2021, December 8, 2021, December 14, 2021, 
May 5, 2022, December 28, 2022, September 
25, 2023 and September 19, 2024 (collectively 
referred to as “MCA Circulars”), permitted 
convening the Annual General Meeting (“AGM” 
/ “Meeting”) through Video Conferencing 
(“VC”) or Other Audio Visual Means (“OAVM”), 
without physical presence of the members 
at a common venue. In accordance with the 
MCA Circulars and applicable provisions of 
the Companies Act, 2013 (“Act”) read with 
Rules made thereunder and the Securities and 
Exchange Board of India (Listing Obligations 
and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations, 
2015 (“Listing Regulations”), the AGM of the 
Company is being held through VC / OAVM. 
The deemed venue for the AGM shall be the 
Corporate office of the Company.
2. In compliance with provisions of  the Act 
read with rules / circulars thereunder and the 
provisions of Listing Regulations read with 
circulars issued thereunder, the Company is 
providing to the Members the facility to exercise 
their right to vote at the AGM by electronic 
means, i.e. remote e-voting and e-voting during 
the AGM (together referred to as “e-voting”).
3. The attendance of the Members attending 
the AGM through VC will be counted for the 
purpose of reckoning the quorum under 
Section 103 of the Act.
4. Since this A GM is being held through VC 
pursuant to the MCA Circulars, physical 
attendance of Members has been dispensed 
with. Accordingly, the facility for appointment 
of proxies by the Members will not be available 
for the AGM. Further, the Route Map of AGM, 
Proxy Form and Attendance Slip are not 
annexed hereto. However, Body Corporates / 
Institutional Members are entitled to appoint 
authorised representatives to attend the AGM 
through VC and cast their votes by electronic 
means.

16.  Voting through electronic means (Remote 
E-voting):
i) In compliance with the provisions of  Section 
108 of the Act, Rule 20 of the Companies 
(Management and Administration) Rules, 
2014 as amended by the Companies 
(Management and Administration) 
Amendment Rules, 2015 and Regulation 
44 of the SEBI Listing Regulations, and 
Secretarial Standard on General Meetings 
(SS-2) issued by the Institute of Company 
Secretaries of India, the Company is pleased 
to provide to its Members the facility to 
exercise their right to vote on resolutions 
proposed to be considered at the 8th AGM 
by electronic means and the business may 
be transacted through e-voting services.
 The facility of casting the votes by the 
Members using an electronic voting system 
from a place other than venue of the AGM 
(“remote e-voting”) will be provided by 
MUFG Intime India Private Limited (MIIPL).
ii) The remote e-voting period commences 
on Tuesday, August 04, 2026 (9:00 a.m. 
IST) and ends on Thursday, August 06, 
2026 (5:00 p.m. IST). During this period, 
Members of the Company holding shares 
either in physical form or in dematerialized 
form, as on the cut-off date (record date) 
of Friday, July 31, 2026 may cast their vote 
electronically. The remote e-voting module 
shall be disabled by MIIPL e-voting platform 
for voting thereafter.
iii) The Members, whose names appear in 
the Register of Members / list of Beneficial 
Owners as on Friday, July 31, 2026, being 
the cut-off date, are entitled to vote on the 
resolutions set forth in this Notice.
iv) The Members who had cast their vote by 
remote e-voting prior to the AGM, may also attend the AGM but shall not be entitled to 
cast their vote again.
v) The facility for e-voting at the AGM will 
be available and the Members attending 
the AGM who have not cast their vote by 
remote e-voting shall be able to exercise 
their right at the AGM through e-voting.
vi) The Company has appointed M/s. 
Sarvaiya & Co. the Company Secretaries as 
Scrutinizer, to scrutinize the entire e-voting 
process in a fair and transparent manner.
vii) The scrutiniser shall, immediately after the 
conclusion of voting at the AGM, first count 
the votes cast at the AGM, thereafter unblock 
the votes cast through remote e-voting in 
the presence of at least two witnesses not in 
the employment of the company and make, 
not later than three days of conclusion of 
the AGM, a consolidated scrutiniser’s report 
of the total votes cast in favor or against, if 
any, to the Chairman or a person authorised 
by him in writing who shall countersign the 
same.
viii) The results declared along with the 
Scrutinizer’s Report shall be placed on 
the Company’s website https://www.
corporate.celloworld.com// and on the 
website of MIIPL immediately after the 
result is declared. The Company shall 
simultaneously forward the results to BSE 
Limited (“BSE”) and The National Stock 
Exchange of India Limited (“NSE”), where 
the shares of the Company are listed.
ix) The Resolutions shall be deemed to be 
passed on the date of AGM i.e. August 07, 
2026 subject to receipt of sufficient votes.

STANDALONE FINANCIAL PREVIEW
The Company’s revenue from operations during the 
financial year under review was `1,11,549.87 Lakhs, as 
against `1,11,262.58 Lakhs in the previous year whereas 
profit of the Company before exceptional items and 
tax was `9,797.96 Lakhs as compared to profit before 
exceptional items and tax of `14,780.49 Lakhs in the 
previous year.
Further, profit of the Company before tax was `9,599.83 
Lakhs as compared to profit before tax of `14,780.49 
Lakhs in the previous year and the Company’s profit for 
the year was `8,049.09 Lakhs compared to profit for the 
year of `11,162.41 Lakhs in the previous year.
CONSOLIDATED FINANCIAL PREVIEW
On consolidated basis, the Company’s revenue from 
operations during the financial year under review was 
`2,32,370.79 Lakhs as against `2,13,638.83 Lakhs in the 
previous year whereas profit of the Company before 
exceptional items and tax was `44,713.62 Lakhs as 
compared to profit before exceptional items and tax of 
`49,128.75 Lakhs in the previous year.
Further, profit of the Company before tax was `43,969.80 
Lakhs as compared to profit before tax of `49,128.75 
Lakhs in the previous year and the Company’s profit for 
the year was `33,150.64 Lakhs compared to profit for the 
year of `36,456.71 Lakhs in the previous year.
TRANSFER TO RESERVES
The company is directing its entire annual profits toward 
growth initiatives, reinforcing its commitment to expansion 
and innovation.
DIVIDEND
Your Directors are pleased to recommend a Dividend of 
`1.5/- (i.e. 30%) per equity share of face value of `5/- each 
for the Financial Year 2025-26 aggregating to a total payout 
of `3,383.07 Lakhs, subject to approval of Members at 
the ensuing 8th Annual General Meeting (“AGM”) of the 
Company and shall be paid within the statutory period to 
those members whose names appear in the register of 
members, holding shares in dematerialized form on the 
close of Friday, July 31, 2026.
The Board of Directors of the Company have approved 
a Dividend Distribution Policy in line with Regulation 43A

Distribution-Policy.pdfMATERIAL CHANGES AND COMMITMENT 
AFFECTING THE FINANCIAL POSITION
The Scheme involving Wim Plast Limited (“WPL”), Cello 
Consumer Products Private Limited (“CCPPL”), and Cello 
World Limited (“CWL”), as approved by the Hon’ble NCLT, 
Ahmedabad Bench, vide its order dated May 14, 2026, 
became effective in May 2026. The consequential effects 
arising from the implementation of the Scheme have been 
incorporated in the Standalone Financial Statements in 
accordance with the applicable Accounting Policies and 
Standards.
MATERIAL EVENTS
In-Principle Approval of Stock Exchange for listing of 
ESOP Shares
During the review period, the Company applied under 
the SEBI (Share Based Employee Benefits) Regulations, 
2021 for the listing of its equity shares under the CELLO 
ESOP-2025 Scheme. The Company subsequently 
secured in-principle approvals from both the National 
Stock Exchange (“NSE”) and the Bombay Stock Exchange 
(“BSE”), where its shares are listed.
Scheme of Arrangement Approval by NCLT
During the period under review, the Scheme of 
Arrangement (“Scheme”) amongst Wim Plast Limited 
(“WPL”), Cello Consumer Products Private Limited 
(“CCPPL”)—a wholly owned subsidiary of Cello World 
Limited (“CWL”)—and Cello World Limited involved in 
the Scheme, was filed before the National Company 
Law Tribunal (NCLT) for approval. The application was 
made after full compliance with Sections 230–232 of the 
Companies Act, 2013, SEBI (LODR) Regulations, 2015, and 
other applicable provisions of the Companies Act, 2013.
Investment in Wholly Owned Subsidiary
During the year, the Company invested a total of `600 
Crore in Cello Consumerware Private Limited (“CCWPL”), 
its wholly owned subsidiary. This comprised `500 Crore 
through conversion of an intercompany loan into equity 
and `100 Crore as fresh capital infusion in cash. The funds 
are being utilized for setting up the manufacturing unit at 
Falna, Rajasthan, working capital requirements, repayment 
of loans, and other general corporate purposes.

RISK MANAGEMENT
The Board established the Risk Management Committee, 
with details regarding its composition and meeting 
frequency outlined in the Corporate Governance Report, 
included in the Annual Report. Additionally, in compliance 
with Section 134(3)(n) of the Act and Regulation 17(9) of 
the SEBI Listing Regulations, the Company has developed 
and implemented a comprehensive Risk Management 
Policy. This policy encompasses procedures for identifying 
potential risks that, in the Board’s assessment, could 
jeopardize the Company’s sustainability.
=== page 38 ===
Annual Report 2025-2652Board’s Report (contd.)
The Risk Management Policy sets out the Company’s 
structured approach to identifying, analysing, prioritizing, 
and mitigating risks, while also ensuring effective risk 
reporting. Applicable across all functions, departments, 
and geographies, the policy aims to establish a robust 
and integrated risk management framework.
The primary objective of the policy is to proactively 
identify, assess, manage, and monitor potential risks 
that could impact the achievement of the Company’s 
strategic and operational objectives. It is designed to 
recognize events that may adversely affect the Company 
and to manage such risks within defined and acceptable 
thresholds, thereby providing reasonable assurance 
towards the realization of corporate goals. The Policy is 
uploaded on the Company’s website at https://corporate.
celloworld.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/02/Risk-
Management-Policy.pdf

professionalism, honesty, integrity, and ethical conduct.

Annual Report 2025-2656Management Discussion And Analysis
Global Economy
Overview 
The global economy remains resilient, as global economy 
grew by 3.4% in 2025. The growth was supported by 
technology-led investments, improving trade flows and 
supportive financial conditions. However, escalating 
geopolitical tensions, especially in the Middle East, 
have introduced fresh headwinds by elevating energy 
prices and affecting global supply chains. Advanced 
economies continue to expand steadily, while emerging 
markets remain more exposed to external shocks. Overall, 
the outlook reflects stable but measured expansion, 
underpinned by structural drivers and tempered by 
geopolitical risks.
2025 2026 (P) 2027 (P)
Global Economy 3.40% 3.10% 3.20%
Inflation Trajectory and Monetary Policy
Global inflation moderated to ~4.1% in 2025 following 
earlier tightening cycles but remains above target levels. 
The escalation of geopolitical tensions in the Middle 
East, particularly the closure of the Strait of Hormuz, has 
triggered a sharp rise in oil prices, creating a supply-
side shock and pushing inflation higher to an estimated 
~4.4% in 2026. Elevated energy costs are feeding into 
transportation, food and industrial prices, reinforcing 
inflationary pressures. Inflation is expected to ease to 
~3.7% in 2027 as supply conditions stabilise. Central 
banks are maintaining a calibrated, data-driven stance, 
balancing inflation control with growth considerations, 
while prioritising anchored expectations and policy 
coheritability.
Inflation trend
Metric 2025 2026 2027
Global Inflation (%) ~4.1% ~4.4% ~3.7%
Regional Growth Dynamics
Growth trends across regions remain uneven but broadly 
resilient, shaped by trade realignments, policy responses, 
and evolving geopolitical developments.
Advanced economies expanded by ~1.9% in 2025 and 
are projected to moderate to ~1.8% in 2026, with a 
marginal improvement to ~1.7% in 2027. Growth remains 
supported by easing financial conditions, fiscal spending, 
and continued investments in technology and digital 
infrastructure. However, structural constraints—including 
ageing demographics, productivity challenges, and tighter fiscal positions—continue to limit acceleration.
Emerging markets and developing economies (EMDEs) 
grew by ~4.4% in 2025 and are expected to sustain 
momentum at ~3.9% in 2026 and ~4.2% in 2027. Asia 
remains a key growth engine, supported by strong 
technology-led exports, manufacturing resilience, and 
supply chain diversification. However, domestic demand 
recovery remains uneven, and commodity-importing 
economies face pressures from elevated energy prices 
and currency volatility.
Sub-Saharan Africa recorded growth of ~4.5% in 2025 
and is projected to improve to ~4.3% in 2026 and ~4.4% 
in 2027. The recovery is supported by easing inflation, 
strengthening consumption, and gradual macroeconomic 
stabilisation. Nonetheless, the region remains exposed 
to external vulnerabilities, including commodity price 
fluctuations, financing constraints, and climate-related 
risks.

and Developing

Outlook
The global economic outlook remains cautiously 
constructive, anchored in resilience but tempered by 
elevated uncertainty. Global growth is projected to 
stabilise at ~3.1% in 2026 and ~3.2% in 2027, following a 
relatively stronger expansion of ~3.4% in 2025. Underlying 
momentum continues to be supported by technology-
driven investments, particularly in digital infrastructure 
and artificial intelligence, alongside steady services trade 
and gradual normalisation of financial conditions.
However, Prolonged geopolitical tensions, intensifying 
geoeconomic fragmentation, and escalating trade 
disputes could materially weaken growth prospects while 
exerting upward pressure on inflation through disrupted 
supply chains, higher input costs, and reduced global 
trade efficiency.
Indian Economy
India retained its position as the world’s fastest-growing 
major economy in FY 2025-26. As per the Provisional 
Estimates released by the Ministry of Statistics and 
Programme Implementation (MoSPI) in June 2026, real 
GDP grew 7.7% during the year, an acceleration from 7.1% 
in FY 2024-25, despite an uncertain global environment. 
Nominal GDP grew 8.9% to reach `346.36 Lakh Crore. Annexure I

oad-based, with real

Growth accelerated through the year, with real GDP rising 
from 6.8% in Q1 to a peak of 8.3% in Q2 FY 2025-26 — the 
highest quarterly growth in six quarters — supported by 
strong domestic demand, healthy consumption trends, 
and improving investment activity. Momentum eased 
marginally to 8.0% in Q3 before settling at 7.8% in Q4 FY 
2025-26, underscoring the resilience of India’s economic 
fundamentals and its sustained growth trajectory through 
the year.

Sectorial Analysis
India’s growth momentum remained robust in FY 2025-26, 
driven by strong industrial activity and resilient services. 
The secondary sector expanded 8.8%, led by 10.7% 
growth in manufacturing, while industrial production 
gained momentum with strong growth in manufacturing 
and core industries. Business confidence remained 
positive, with the manufacturing PMI staying in expansion 
territory and medium- and high-technology industries 
accounting for 46.3% of manufacturing value added.
The services sector continued to anchor economic 
growth, with GVA rising 9.3% (vs. 7.9% in FY 2024-25) 
and contributing 54.3% of nominal GVA. Growth was 
supported by resilient domestic consumption, steady 
expansion in trade, transport and communication services, 
and stable services exports.
Taming Inflation
India’s inflation remained well contained during FY 2025-
26, providing a stable macroeconomic backdrop. Retail 
inflation eased to 3.40% in March 2026 and stood at 
3.48% in April 2026, remaining comfortably within the 
RBI’s target range. Moderating food prices, particularly vegetables, helped contain inflation despite continued 
price pressures in select categories such as personal care 
and precious metals.
In response to easing inflation, the RBI adopted a calibrated 
accommodative stance, reducing the repo rate to 5.25% 
and the Cash Reserve Ratio (CRR) to 3%. These measures 
improved liquidity, strengthened credit transmission and 
supported economic growth. Looking ahead, inflation is 
expected to remain around the RBI’s 4% target, although 
geopolitical developments and monsoon conditions will 
continue to influence the inflation outlook.

Outlook
India’s medium-term growth outlook remains robust, 
with real GDP projected to grow in the range of 6.8%–
7.2% in FY 2026-27. This trajectory is underpinned by 
resilient domestic demand, sustained government capital 
expenditure, and continued strength in manufacturing 
and services. Ongoing improvements in infrastructure 
and the investment climate are expected to further 
support economic expansion.
Over the longer term, India’s growth is anchored in the 
Government’s Viksit Bharat vision, which aims to position 
the country as a developed economy by 2047, targeting a 
GDP of USD 30–40 trillion and per capita income of USD 
15,000–18,000. While the outlook remains constructive, 
global trade disruptions, geopolitical risks, and external 
volatility may pose challenges, necessitating continued 
policy prudence and macroeconomic stability.

Indian Consumerware Market
Overview
The Indian consumerware market has expanded steadily, 
growing from USD 1.62 billion in 2015 to USD 3.38 billion 
in 2025E, reflecting strong structural demand. Growth 
has been driven by rising incomes, urbanisation, and 
evolving lifestyle preferences, with increasing demand 
for organised and functional kitchen solutions. Changing 
consumption patterns, higher product ownership, and 
a shift towards aesthetically appealing and utility-driven 
products continue to support market expansion.

Indian Stationary Market
Overview
The Indian stationery industry in 2025 has a value of 
around `30,000 Crore. Supported by strong demand 
from the education ecosystem and growing office 
consumption. Rising literacy, increasing enrolments, 
and expansion of institutions have been key demand 
drivers. Urbanisation, improving incomes, and a shift 
towards premium and personalised products are further 
supporting market growth. The rise of e-commerce has 
also enhanced accessibility, particularly in tier 2 and tier 3 
cities, strengthening market penetration.
Key Market Drivers
Strengthening Government Focus on Education
Government initiatives continue to strengthen India’s 
education ecosystem, supporting long-term demand for 
stationery products. The Union Budget 2025-26 allocated 
~USD 1,503 to the Ministry of Education, reflecting a 6.22% 
increase over the previous year. Flagship programmes 
such as SSA, RMSA and NEP 2020, along with initiatives 
like Skills India and Digital India, are expanding access to 
education, improving enrolments and driving demand for 
a wider range of educational and stationery products.
Rise of Remote Work Culture
The growing adoption of remote and hybrid work models 
has expanded stationery demand beyond traditional 
office environments. As of 2024, approximately 12.7% 
of full-time employees worked remotely, while 28.2% 
followed a hybrid work model, increasing demand for 
home-office supplies such as planners, notebooks and 
organisational tools. E-commerce platforms have further 
strengthened product accessibility, supporting higher 
consumption across personal workspaces.Expansion of Corporate Sector
India’s expanding corporate and startup ecosystem 
continues to create significant opportunities for the 
stationery industry. During FY 2025-26, the Government 
recognised more than 55,200 startups—the highest 
number recognised in a single year since the launch of 
the Startup India initiative. In addition, 375 companies 
were listed during 2025, driving demand for branded 
and customised stationery, including pens, notebooks 
and planners, to support corporate branding, 
employee engagement and evolving hybrid workplace 
requirements.

Key Challenges
Volatility in Raw Material Prices
Fluctuations in the cost of paper, plastics, and ink—driven 
by global supply chain disruptions—impact pricing, 
margins, and profitability for manufacturers.
High Market Fragmentation and Competition
The presence of multiple domestic and international 
players has intensified competition, leading to pricing 
pressures and margin compression across the industry.
Pressure from Low-Cost Imports 
Increasing imports, particularly from cost-competitive 
markets, challenge domestic players to maintain quality 
while remaining price competitive.

Indian Non Paper Stationary Market
The Indian non-paper stationery market—comprising 
writing instruments (pens, pencils, markers), art supplies 
and related products—continues to exhibit strong growth 
momentum, supported by structural demand from the 
education sector and rising formalisation. While precise 
market sizing varies across sources, the category forms 
a significant portion of the overall stationery industry 
and is expected to grow at a CAGR of ~8–8.50% over 
the medium term (2023-29), outperforming the broader 
stationery category.
https://www.6wresearch.com/industry-report/india-non-
paper-stationery-market
Growth in the category is primarily driven by increasing 
school enrolments, expansion of the education ecosystem 
and rising demand from offices and corporate users. The 
educational category remains the dominant end-user, 
accounting for most of the consumption, while office and 
institutional demand continue to provide incremental 
growth support. Writing instruments, particularly pens, 
constitute the largest revenue-contributing category 
within non-paper stationery, reflecting their essential 
usage across both academic and professional settings.Overall, the Indian non-paper stationery market presents 
a strong growth outlook, supported by favourable 
demographics, increasing literacy rates and evolving 
consumer preferences, positioning branded players to 
benefit from sustained demand and ongoing market 
consolidation.
Indian Writing Instrument Market
The Indian writing instruments market was valued at 
USD 1,802.6 million in 2025 and is projected to witness 
robust expansion, reaching approximately USD 2,587.3 
million by 2033, registering a compound annual growth 
rate (CAGR) of 4.7% over the forecast period. This strong 
growth trajectory is primarily driven by India’s large 
and expanding youth population, which continues to 
underpin sustained demand for writing instruments 
across educational and early professional segments. 
Additionally, increasing enrolment levels, rising literacy 
rates, and growing preference for branded and premium 
products are expected to further support market 
expansion over the long term.

positive, supported by favourable demographics, rising 
literacy levels, and sustained investments in education 
infrastructure. The market is expected to benefit from 
premiumisation trends, growing adoption of branded 
products, and expansion into Tier II and Tier III markets.
Further, the rapid growth of e-commerce and modern 
retail channels is likely to enhance accessibility and drive 
volume growth, while innovation in product design and 
materials is expected to support value growth. Overall, the 
industry is well-positioned for long-term expansion, with 
strong underlying demand fundamentals and increasing 
formalisation of the market.

consumer interest in personalised and handmade goods, 
and an increasing variety of available craft materials.

e-commerce platforms, a notable shift in consumer

Indian Furniture Market
The Indian furniture industry is witnessing strong growth, 
with the market expected to expand from USD 29.27 
billion in 2025 to USD 31.51 billion in 2026, supported 
by rising urbanisation, higher disposable incomes, and 
growth in real estate. Structural drivers such as housing 
initiatives, increasing urban households, and MSME 
formalisation are expanding demand, particularly in mid-
range segments.
Regulatory measures like the Furniture Quality Control 
Order (February 2026) are improving product standards 
and encouraging formalisation, while government 
procurement platforms are boosting institutional demand. 
India remains one of the fastest-growing furniture markets 
globally, with the residential category contributing over 
~70% of demand and increasing traction in office and 
institutional furniture. The market continues to transition 
towards organised and branded players, supported by 
e-commerce growth and rising preference for quality and 
sustainable products.

Market Drivers
Urbanisation and Migration-led Demand
Rising urbanisation is driving furniture demand, with 
the urban population expected to exceed 40% by 2030 
(from 34.5% in 2021). Around 18.9% of internal migrants 
move to cities, leading to new household formation 
and increased demand for affordable, space-efficient 
furniture, particularly in tier-II cities.Omnichannel and Digital Adoption
The shift towards omnichannel retail is transforming the 
market, with AR/VR tools improving purchase decisions 
and reducing returns. Platforms like ONDC and GeM are 
expanding market access for MSMEs, while e-commerce 
and digital integration are enhancing reach and supply 
chain efficiency.
Government Housing-led Demand
Housing initiatives such as PMAY have significantly 
expanded the base of first-time buyers, with over 96 Lakh 
urban and 2.92 Crore rural homes completed. The push 
towards affordable housing continues to drive demand 
for basic and functional furniture.
Sustainability and Material Shift
Growing focus on sustainability is driving adoption of 
engineered wood, low-VOC finishes, and eco-certified 
products. IGBC guidelines and quality regulations are 
further strengthening demand for environmentally 
compliant furniture, particularly in premium and 
institutional segments.
Key Challenges
Price Sensitivity and GST Impact
Furniture demand in India remains highly price-sensitive, 
with durable goods accounting for a limited share of 
household spending. The prevailing 18% GST on most 
furniture categories elevates end prices, restricting 
adoption of premium products, particularly among lower- 
and middle-income consumers. While certain materials 
like bamboo benefit from lower tax slabs, the broader 
category continues to face pricing constraints. Despite 
some improvement in disposable incomes, the transition 
towards premium furniture—such as solid wood and 
high-end seating—remains gradual due to affordability 
considerations.
Supply Chain Constraints and Input Volatility
The furniture industry continues to face supply-side 
challenges, particularly in sourcing certified timber. 
Dependence on a mix of domestic and imported wood, 
coupled with traceability issues and stringent compliance 
requirements, leads to cost fluctuations and procurement 
delays. Regulatory processes, including quarantine norms 
and documentation for imported timber, add to lead times 
and complexity, especially for MSMEs. Additionally, price 
=== page 48 ===

volatility in k
ey hardwoods and evolving trade regulations 
impact production planning and delivery timelines across 
the value chain.

Outlook
The Indian furniture market is poised for steady expansion, 
projected to grow from USD 31.51 billion in 2026 to 
USD 45.52 billion by 2031, at a CAGR of 7.63%. Growth 
will be supported by rising urbanisation, increasing 
housing demand, and evolving consumer preferences 
towards organised and branded products. Additionally, 
expansion in office infrastructure, institutional demand, 
and continued adoption of omnichannel retail are 
expected to sustain momentum across both residential 
and commercial segments.

COMPANY OVERVIEW
We are Cello World Limited, one of India’s leading 
consumer products companies, with a brand legacy 
spanning over six decades since 1962. We have 
established ourselves as a trusted household brand, 
offering a diverse portfolio across consumer houseware, 
writing instruments and stationery, and moulded furniture 
and allied products. Our product range spans everyday 
essentials to premium lifestyle offerings, catering to a 
wide spectrum of consumer needs across price points.Our strong market position is underpinned by our focus 
on innovation, design-led functionality, and affordability, 
enabling us to remain relevant to evolving consumer 
preferences. With over 20,000 + SKUs and continuous 
product innovation, including the launch of ~2000+ new 
SKUs in FY 2025-26, we have strengthened our presence 
across both core and emerging categories.
We operate an extensive distribution network, serving 
over 1.9+ Lakh retailers through 5000 distributors, 
complemented by a growing presence in modern trade, 
e-commerce, and international markets. Our robust 
manufacturing backbone, supported by 14 state-of-the-
art facilities across India, enables strong control over 
quality, cost efficiency, and scalability.
Listed on the NSE and BSE in 2023, we continue to 
strengthen our brand equity and expand our product 
portfolio, driven by a customer-centric approach, strong 
operational capabilities, and a commitment to delivering 
innovative, high-quality products that seamlessly integrate 
into everyday life.

Risk Impact Mitigation Measures
Macroeconomic
FactorsMacroeconomic conditions, including 
inflationary pressures, interest rate 
movements, and global uncertainties, may 
influence demand patterns, input costs, and 
our overall profitability.We maintain a diversified product portfolio 
comprising both essential and discretionary 
categories, thereby reducing sensitivity to 
economic fluctuations. We continuously 
evaluate pricing strategies in line with 
prevailing market conditions. Additionally, 
targeted promotions, discounts, and 
consumer schemes support demand across 
segments.
Raw Material Price Risk Raw material costs form a major share of our 
expenditure and are influenced by factors 
such as policy changes, regulatory shifts and 
global developments. Variations in input 
prices may adversely affect profitability.We  actively  monitor  input cost trends and 
optimises inventory management to mitigate 
price fluctuations. We focus on operational 
efficiencies to manage cost pressures. Where 
required, calibrated price adjustments are 
undertaken to partially offset increases in raw 
material costs.
Liquidity Risk Liquidity management involves careful 
control of funds to meet operational and 
statutory obligations on time. It also requires 
maintaining adequate cash reserves and 
access to bank credit for unforeseen needs.We follow a disciplined approach to cash 
flow management, supported by continuous 
monitoring of financial requirements. 
Adequate cash reserves and access to 
committed banking facilities provide financial 
flexibility. We also maintain contingency plans 
to address unforeseen funding requirements.
Distribution Network 
RiskWe utilised our distribution channels across 
domestic and international markets to ensure 
efficient product reach. Inability to maintain 
and strengthen this network may hinder 
product availability, potentially resulting in 
reduced market presence and share.We continue to expand and diversify our 
distribution network by onboarding new 
partners across geographies. This reduces 
dependency on individual distributors 
while enhancing market coverage. Existing 
distributors are supported through incentive 
programmes and engagement initiatives to 
improve performance and loyalty.
Competition Risk The existence of informal players and 
heightened rivalry within the sector creates 
considerable pressure. Increased competition 
may compress profit margins, as we may 
be required to reduce pricing to remain 
competitive.Our broad product portfolio across multiple 
price points, along with a strong focus on 
quality, enables us to differentiate ourselves. 
This positioning supports sustained 
competitiveness in a dynamic market 
environment.
Counterfeit Risk Unauthorized market players may produce 
and distribute imitation versions of our 
products, potentially harming our brand 
integrity and public perception.With a legacy spanning over six decades, we 
have  established  strong brand recognition. 
We undertake continuous brand protection 
initiatives, including awareness campaigns 
and enforcement measures, to safeguard 
brand integrity and mitigate counterfeiting 
risks.

Risk Impact Mitigation Measures
Quality Risk Quality risk represents the likelihood that 
products or services may fail to meet 
established standards, potentially affecting 
brand credibility and financial outcomes. 
This exposure may arise from production 
flaws, weak oversight mechanisms, or 
interruptions in sourcing and logistics. If not 
effectively mitigated, such risks can result in 
negative customer experiences, withdrawal 
of offerings from the market, and increased 
legal or regulatory consequences.We have implemented stringent quality 
control systems across all stages of 
production. Dedicated teams oversee 
adherence to defined standards, with regular 
inspections conducted across sourcing, 
manufacturing, and packaging processes to 
ensure consistent product quality.
Change in Customer 
PreferenceChanges in customer and consumer 
preferences may negatively affect demand.We actively monitor evolving market trends 
and explores opportunities to introduce new 
offerings aligned with customer needs.

Cello World Limited65Management Discussion And Analysis   (contd.)
Sales Distribution Network
We have established a scaled and resilient pan-India sales 
and distribution network, comprising 5000+ distributors 
and 1,80,000+ retailers, supported by a field force of 
740+ sales personnel. This extensive reach underpins 
consistent market access, strong last-mile connectivity, 
and effective execution across geographies, with a 
category-aligned distribution model enabling targeted 
penetration.
We have diversified channel mix, led by General Trade 
(~74.9%) and supported by Online (~10.50%), Exports 
(~9.9%), and Modern Trade (~6.70%), provides balanced 
exposure to traditional and emerging consumption 
channels, enhancing demand capture and reducing 
channel concentration risk.
Operationally, the network is strengthened by integrated 
ERP systems and real-time secondary sales tracking, 
enabling accurate demand forecasting, improved 
inventory turns, and data-led decision-making. This 
technology-driven distribution framework enhances 
agility, supports scalable growth, and positions us to 
effectively respond to evolving consumer preferences 
and market dynamics.
Information Technology
We have adopted advanced digital systems and 
integrated automation across our design, manufacturing, 
and distribution operations to improve efficiency and 
optimise costs while maintaining quality standards. Our 
automated production facilities enable scalable, high-
volume output at competitive cost levels. An Enterprise 
Resource Planning (ERP) system has been implemented 
across key segments and is being progressively extended 
to other business areas, facilitating seamless coordination, 
enhanced visibility, and data-driven decision-making. The 
system also supports supply chain oversight, demand 
monitoring, and efficient inventory management.
We have continued to upgrade the technology 
infrastructure to drive productivity and operational 
excellence. The sales function leverages digital tools to 
track real-time market trends and enable agile expansion 
strategies aligned with evolving consumer preferences. 
Additionally, we have strengthened our information 
security framework through enhanced email protection 
systems, robust cybersecurity protocols, and ongoing 
employee awareness and training programs.Human Resource
We consider our workforce a critical source of competitive 
strength. Our employees possess diverse industry 
experience, technical capabilities, and strong functional 
expertise. The organisational culture promotes innovation 
and encourages progressive thinking to enhance overall 
performance. We remain committed to aligning business 
objectives with the professional and personal aspirations 
of our employees, fostering a supportive environment 
and a healthy work-life balance.
As of March 31, 2026, we had a workforce of 3116 
employees, with women representing 20% of the total. 
The attrition rate on a Last Twelve Months (LTM) basis 
stood at 19% for 2025-26, reflecting an improvement 
from 20.12% in the previous year.
Internal Control System and its Adequacy
We have established a comprehensive system to ensure 
the utmost accuracy in all operations and 64 effective 
management of potential risks. This encompasses robust 
monitoring of our financial assets to safeguard them from 
unauthorized access. A dedicated team conducts regular 
inspections to ensure compliance, while a robust system 
accurately tracks expenses. Such an efficient system is 
vital for our success and bolsters stakeholder confidence 
in meeting regulatory and legal requirements.
Cautionary Statement
The statements in the Management Discussion and 
Analysis section describing our objectives, projections, 
estimates, and predictions may be considered forward 
looking statements. All statements that address 
expectations or predictions about the future, including, but 
not limited to, statements about our strategy for growth, 
product development, market positioning, expenditures, 
and financial results, are based on certain assumptions 
and expectations of future events. We cannot guarantee 
that these assumptions and expectations are accurate 
or will be realised. Our actual results, performance, or 
achievements may thus differ materially from those 
projected in such forward-looking statements. We assume 
no responsibility to publicly amend, modify, or revise any 
forward-looking statement based on any subsequent 
developments, information, or events. To avoid 
duplication and repetition, certain heads of information 
required to be disclosed in the Management Discussion 
and Analysis have been included in the Board’s Report.

1.  Company’s Philosophy on Code of Governance
Corporate governance forms the foundation of our 
company’s operations, shaping our actions to uphold 
transparency, accountability, and ethical integrity across 
all business functions. This report presents our corporate 
governance framework, practices, and initiatives, all 
designed to foster long-term value creation for our 
stakeholders.
Our Company is committed to conducting its business in a 
fair and responsible manner, in alignment with our mission 
to provide quality consumer products at affordable prices. 
In doing so, we strive to deliver sustainable, long-term 
value to our shareholders.
The Board firmly believes that the principles of corporate 
governance should extend beyond statutory obligations 
to include a strong sense of social responsibility. As 
our organization grows, we are equally committed to 
contributing to the growth and well-being of the society 
and communities in which we operate.REPORT ON  
CORPORATE GOVERNANCEANNEXURE - III
2.  Board of Directors
Our Board is composed of a diverse and experienced 
group of professionals, each bringing deep expertise 
across areas critical to our business. Together, they 
provide strategic oversight, guide the company’s long-
term direction, and ensure that management operates in 
the best interests of our shareholders and stakeholders.
The Company’s Board of Directors maintains an optimal 
balance of Executive and Non-Executive Directors in 
alignment with corporate governance best practices. 
The Board comprises of nine Directors, including three 
Executive Directors—one serving as the Chairman and 
Managing Director and other two as the Joint Managing 
Director. The remaining six are Non-Executive Directors, 
of which five are Independent Directors, including two 
Independent Women Directors.
The composition of the Board complies with the 
requirements of Regulation 17 of the SEBI Listing 
Regulations, 2015 and Section 149 of the Companies 
Act, 2013 (“the Act”), ensuring adherence to statutory 
obligations and governance standards.

3.  Audit Committee
a.    Broad terms of reference of the Audit Committee 
are as per following:
The role of the Audit Committee shall include the 
following: 
(1) oversight of financial r eporting process and the 
disclosure of financial information relating to the 
Company to ensure that the financial statements are 
correct, sufficient and credible;
(2) recommendation to the Board for appointment, re-
appointment, replacement, remuneration and terms 
of appointment of auditors of the Company and the 
fixation of the audit fee;
(3) approval of payment to statutory auditors for any 
other services rendered by the statutory auditors;
(4) formulation of  a policy on related party transactions, 
which shall include materiality of related party 
transactions;
(5) reviewing, at least on a quarterly basis, the details of 
related party transactions entered by the Company 
pursuant to each of the omnibus approvals given;
(6) examining and reviewing, with the management, 
the annual financial statements and auditor’s report 
thereon before submission to the Board for approval, 
with particular reference to: 
a. Matters required to be included in the director’s 
responsibility statement to be included in the 
Board’s report in terms of clause (c) of sub-
section 3 of section 134 of the Companies Act, 
2013;
b. Changes, if  any, in accounting policies and 
practices and reasons for the same;
c. Major accounting entries involving estimates 
based on the exercise of judgment by 
management;
d. Significant adjustments made in the financial 
statements arising out of audit findings;
e. Compliance with listing and other legal 
requirements relating to financial statements; 
f. Disclosure of  any related party transactions; and 
g. Modified opinion(s) in the draf t audit report. 
(7) reviewing, with the management, the quarterly, 
half-yearly and annual financial statements before 
submission to the Board for approval;
(8) reviewing, with the management, the statement of 
uses / application of funds raised through an issue 
(public issue, rights issue, preferential issue, etc.), the 
statement of funds utilized for purposes other than 
those stated in the Offer document / prospectus / 
notice and the report submitted by the monitoring agency monitoring the utilisation of proceeds of a 
public or rights issue or preferential issue or qualified 
institutions placement, and making appropriate 
recommendations to the Board to take up steps in 
this matter; 
(9) reviewing and monitoring the auditor’s 
independence and performance, and effectiveness 
of audit process;
(10) approval of any subsequent modification of 
transactions of the Company with related parties 
and omnibus approval for related party transactions 
proposed to be entered into by the Company, 
subject to the conditions as may be prescribed; 
 Explanation: The term “related party transactions” 
shall have the same meaning as provided in 
Regulation 2(zc) of the SEBI Listing Regulations and /
or the applicable Accounting Standards and/or the 
Companies Act, 2013.
(11) scrutiny of inter -corporate loans and investments;
(12) valuation of undertakings or assets of the Company, 
wherever it is necessary;
(13) evaluation of  internal financial controls and risk 
management systems;
(14) reviewing, with the management, performance of 
statutory and internal auditors, adequacy of the 
internal control systems;
(15) reviewing the adequacy of internal audit function, 
if any, including the structure of the internal audit 
department, staffing and seniority of the official 
heading the department, reporting structure 
coverage and frequency of internal audit;
(16) discussion with internal auditors of any significant 
findings and follow up there on;
(17) reviewing the findings of any internal investigations 
by the internal auditors into matters where there is 
suspected fraud or irregularity or a failure of internal 
control systems of a material nature and reporting 
the matter to the Board;
(18) discussion with statutory auditors befor e the audit 
commences, about the nature and scope of audit as 
well as post-audit discussion to ascertain any area of 
concern;
(19) looking into the re asons for substantial defaults 
in the payment to depositors, debenture holders, 
shareholders (in case of non-payment of declared 
dividends) and creditors;
(20) reviewing the functioning of the whistle blower 
mechanism;
(21) establishing a vigil mechanism for directors and 
employees to report their genuine concerns or 
=== page 58 ===
Annual Report 2025-2672grievances;
(22) overseeing the vigil mechanism established by the 
Company, with the chairman of the Audit Committee 
directly hearing grievances of victimization of 
employees and directors, who used vigil mechanism 
to report genuine concerns in appropriate and 
exceptional cases;
(23) approval of appointment of chief financial officer 
(i.e., the whole-time finance Director or any other 
person heading the finance function or discharging 
that function) after assessing the qualifications, 
experience and background, etc. of the candidate;
(24) approve the disclosure of the key performance 
indicators to be disclosed in the documents in 
relation to the initial public offering of the equity 
shares of the Company;
(25) carrying out any other functions requir ed to be 
carried out by the Audit Committee as contained in 
the SEBI Listing Regulations or any other applicable 
law, as and when amended from time to time;
(26) reviewing the utilization of loans and/or advances 
from / investment by the holding company in the 
subsidiary exceeding ₹1,000,000,000 or 10% of 
the asset size of the subsidiary, whichever is lower 
including existing loans / advances / investments 
existing; 
(27) Considering and commenting on rationale, cost-
benefits and impact of schemes involving merger, 
demerger, amalgamation etc., on the listed entity 
and its shareholders; and
(28) To carry out such other functions as may be specified by the Board of Directors from time to time or 
specified/provided under the Companies Act or the 
SEBI Listing Regulations or by any other regulatory 
authority.
It may be clarified that the power, role and review of the 
Audit Committee includes matters specified under Part 
C of Schedule II of the SEBI Listing Regulations, 2015 
as amended from time to time, as applicable to the

a.    Brief description of terms of reference & role of 
Nomination and Remuneration Committee, inter-
alia includes the following
(1) Formulation of the criteria for determining 
qualifications, positive attributes and independence 
of a director and recommend to the board of 
=== page 59 ===
Statutory reports
Cello World Limited73directors of the Company (the “Board” or “Board of 
Directors”) a policy relating to the remuneration of 
the directors, key managerial personnel and other 
employees (“Remuneration Policy”).
 The Nomination and Remuneration Committee, 
while formulating the above policy, should ensure 
that:
(i) For every appointment of an independent 
director, it shall evaluate the balance of skills, 
knowledge and experience on the Board and 
on the basis of such evaluation, prepare a 
description of the role and capabilities required 
of an independent director. The person 
recommended to the Board for appointment 
as an independent director shall have the 
capabilities identified in such description. For 
the purpose of identifying suitable candidates, 
the Nomination and Remuneration Committee 
may:
a) use the services of an e xternal agencies, if 
required;
b) consider candidates fr om a wide range of 
backgrounds, having due regard to diversity; 
and
c) consider the time commitments of the  
candidates.
(ii) the level and composition of r emuneration be 
reasonable and sufficient to attract, retain and 
motivate directors of the quality required to run 
our Company successfully;
(iii) relationship of remuneration to performance 
is clear and meets appropriate performance 
benchmarks; and 
(iv) remuneration to directors, key managerial 
personnel and senior management involves 
a balance between fixed and incentive pay 
reflecting short- and long-term performance 
objectives appropriate to the working of the 
Company and its goals.
(2) Formulation of criteria for evaluation of 
independent directors and the Board;
(3) Devising a policy on diversity of the Board;
(4) Identifying persons who are qualified to become 
directors and who may be appointed in senior 
management in accordance with the criteria 
laid down, and recommend to the Board their 
appointment and removal and carrying out 
evaluation of every director’s performance (including independent director);
(5) Analysing, monitoring and r eviewing various human 
resource and compensation matters;
(6) Deciding whether to extend or continue the term 
of appointment of the independent director, on the 
basis of the report of performance evaluation of 
independent directors;
(7) Determining the Company’ s policy on specific 
remuneration packages for executive directors 
including pension rights and any compensation 
payment, and determining remuneration packages 
of such directors;
(8) Recommending to the board, all remuneration, in 
whatever form, payable to senior management and 
other staff, as deemed necessary;
 Explanation: The expression senior management

6.  Risk Management Committee
The terms of reference of the Risk Management 
Committee include:
1. To formulate a detailed risk management policy 
which shall include:
(a) A framework for identification of internal and 
external risks specifically faced by the Company, 
in particular including financial, operational, 
sectoral, sustainability (particularly, ESG related 
risks), information, cyber security risks or any 
other risk as may be determined by the Risk 
Management Committee.
(b) Measures for risk mitigation including systems 
and processes for internal control of identified 
risks.
(c) Business continuity plan.
2. To ensure that appropriate methodology, processes 
and systems are in place to monitor and evaluate 
risks associated with the business of the Company;
3. To monitor and oversee implementation of the 
risk management policy, including evaluating the 
adequacy of risk management systems; 
4. To periodically review the risk management policy, at least once in two years, including by considering 
the changing industry dynamics and evolving 
complexity; 
5. To keep the board of directors informed about 
the nature and content of its discussions, 
recommendations and actions to be taken; 
6. The appointment, removal and terms of remuneration 
of the Chief Risk Officer (if any) shall be subject to 
review by the Risk Management Committee; 
7. To review and assess the risk management system 
and policy of the Company from time to time and 
recommend for amendment or modification thereof;
8. To review and recommend potential risk involved in 
any new business plans and processes;
9. To review the Company’s risk-reward performance to 
align with the Company’s overall policy objectives;
10. To seek information from any employee, obtain 
outside legal or other professional advice and secure 
attendance of outsiders with relevant expertise, if it 
considers necessary. 
11. Advise the Board with r egard to risk management 
decisions in relation to strategic and operational 
matters such as corporate strategy; 
12. Coordination of activities with other committee,

2 Does the entity have procedures 
in place for sustainable sourcing? 
(Yes/No)Cello does not have a formal procedure in place for sustainable sourcing 
however we follow certain practises :
1. We source our primary materials through subsidiaries, implementing strict

standards.

3 Describe the processes in place to safely reclaim your products for reusing, recycling and disposing at the 
end of life, for:
Plastics (including packaging) Cello Group has implemented an internal initiative across several of its 
manufacturing facilities to responsibly manage Low-Density Polyethylene 
(LDPE) waste. Under this initiative, LDPE waste is sent to authorized third-
party processors, where it is recycled and converted into poly bags that are 
subsequently utilized for various internal applications. This practice supports 
resource efficiency and promotes the circular use of materials within the 
organization.
As part of its broader commitment to responsible waste management, the 
Group has also adopted measures such as prioritizing the procurement of 
recyclable electronic equipment and partnering with certified recyclers for 
the environmentally sound disposal and recycling of waste. These initiatives 
reflect the Company’s ongoing efforts to minimize environmental impact and 
strengthen sustainable operational practices.E-waste
Hazardous waste
Other waste

4 Does the entity have an equal opportunity policy as per the

web-link to the policy.The Company’s Equal Employment Opportunity Policy 
is founded on the principles of fairness, inclusivity, and 
non-discrimination. The policy is designed to provide 
equal employment opportunities to all employees 
and applicants, irrespective of gender, age, social 
origin, religion, beliefs, disability, race, colour, genetic 
information, protected veteran status, or any other legally 
protected characteristic.
The Company is committed to fostering a diverse and 
inclusive workplace where all individuals are treated with 
dignity and respect and have equal access to opportunities 
for growth and development. The policy is communicated 
to employees and is accessible through the Company’s 
intranet portal.

10 Health and safety management system:
AWhether an occupational 
health and safety management 
system has been implemented 
by the entity? (Yes/ No). If yes, 
the coverage such system?The company has formulated an environmental health and safety 
policy that covers all employees, contractors, and operations across 
all locations. The system focuses on hazard and risk management, 
preventive measures, and incident reporting. It also includes 
disciplinary actions for safety violations to ensure compliance and 
maintain a safe work environment.
bWhat are the processes used to 
identify work-related hazards 
and assess risks on a routine 
and non-routine basis by the 
entity?To identify work-related hazards and assess risks, Cello World and its 
subsidiaries follow a comprehensive process
1. R egular Inspections: Conduct regular workplace inspections to 
identify hazards such as unsafe conditions (e.g., faulty equipment, 
slippery floors) and unsafe practices (e.g., improper techniques).
2. Maintenance and Assessments: Perform periodic assessments and 
maintenance of machinery, plant, and equipment to ensure safety 
and efficiency.
3. Systematic Hazard Identification: Implement a systematic approach 
to identify hazards associated with specific processes or job roles, 
assess the associated risks, and apply controls to mitigate them.
4. Risk Assessment for Changes: Assess risks associated with changes 
in equipment, processes, or procedures before implementation, 
including the evaluation of new technologies and facilities.
cWhether you have processes 
for workers to report the work-
related hazards and to remove 
themselves from such risks. 
(Y/N)According to the policy, Cello has a structured protocol for employees 
to report occupational injuries, accidents, and near misses to the safety 
officer or their supervisor immediately. This ensures that hazards are 
promptly addressed and mitigated, allowing workers to remove 
themselves from such risks.
dDo the employees/ worker of 
the entity have access to non-
occupational medical and 
healthcare services? (Yes/ No)Yes, Cello, at subsidiary plant level, conducts monthly routine medical 
check-up.
At the Cello corporate level, yoga sessions and eye check-ups are 
conducted to promote the wellbeing of the employees Business Responsibility and Sustainability Report (contd.)

1 Describe the processes for 
identifying key stakeholder 
groups of the entity.To identify key stakeholder groups, we employ a systematic approach to 
understanding who is impacted by or has an interest in our organization’s 
activities, decisions, and outcomes. The process includes:
1.  Engage with various internal departments to identify relevant stakeholders.

government authorities, local communities, NGOs).
3. Categorize stakeholders by their level of influence and interest in the 
organization’s activities.

5Describe the internal mechanisms in place to 
redress grievances related to human rights 
issues.The company’s Employee Grievance Redressal Policy 
provides a structured process for employees to voice 
and resolve grievances constructively. Employees can 
file grievances related to workplace harassment, health 
and safety, supervisor behaviour, and adverse changes in 
employment conditions. The procedure involves multiple 
levels, starting with reporting to the immediate manager 
and escalating to the HR department if necessary. The HR 
department is responsible for investigating grievances, 
ensuring confidentiality, and keeping all parties informed 
throughout the process. The policy also ensures non-
retaliation and equal treatment for all employees who file 
grievances.

8 Mechanisms to prevent adverse consequences 
to the complainant in discrimination and 
harassment cases.Cello’s Employee grievance policy includes mechanisms to protect 
complainants in discrimination and harassment cases by ensuring 
strict confidentiality, enforcing a non-retaliation policy, providing a 
structured grievance procedure with multiple oversight levels, and 
guaranteeing equal treatment for all employees who file grievances. 
These measures collectively prevent any adverse consequences for 
the complainant, ensuring their rights and well-being are safeguarded 
throughout the process.
Cello’s POSH Policy states that to prevent adverse consequences for 
complainants in discrimination and harassment cases, they ensure 
strict confidentiality throughout the investigation process. The 
company prohibits any form of retaliation against complainants or 
witnesses who report incidents. Measures include transferring either 
party to different locations if necessary, granting additional leave to 
the complainant, and preventing the respondent from assessing the 
complainant’s work performance during the inquiry. These actions 
help maintain a safe and supportive environment for all parties 
involved
